Reviewer work in this\n 11|module applies only when `directive.reviewer` is present.\n 12|\n 13|### Construction Bolt gates (walking skeleton + ladder + halt-and-ask)\n 14|\n 15|> **Status — two layers.** Follow the shipped layer; do not run the planned\n 16|> Bolt-major layer as conductor procedure.\n 17|>\n 18|> **Shipped:** walking-skeleton *stance* classification (`gate: \"unresolved\"`\n 19|> in the harness bindings; resolution order `org.md` → `team.md` →\n 20|> `project.md`), the first Construction EXECUTE-stage gate\n 21|> (`isSkeletonGateStage`), the ladder prompt after that gate, halt-and-ask on\n 22|> Code Generation failure (including swarm/worktree `BOLT_FAILED`), and the\n 23|> Build-and-Test loop-back sibling below. `BOLT_STARTED` / `BOLT_COMPLETED`\n 24|> fire only on the swarm / worktree path. A default gated run does not record\n 25|> them.\n 26|>\n 27|> **Non-executable future-state:** any instruction in this subsection to treat\n 28|> a Bolt as one pass through 3.1–3.5, to gate a Bolt's combined design\n 29|> artifacts and generated code, to emit `BOLT_COMPLETED` on the default gated\n 30|> walk, or to present subsequent Bolt-level / per-Bolt-batch gates. The\n 31|> default walk is stage-major; runtime batches come from\n 32|> `unit-of-work-dependency.md`. The planned ceremony is kept here as design\n 33|> intent until a later walk consumes `bolt-plan.md`.\n 34|\n 35|Construction introduces a walking-skeleton stage gate, a one-time ladder, and halt-and-ask on Code Generation failure. The shipped walk is the **Engine-driven per-unit iteration** block later in this module. Planned Bolt-major variants are marked below.\n 36|\n 37|**Walking-skeleton gate (first in-scope Construction EXECUTE stage)**\n 38|\n 39|When the resolved Unit DAG is non-empty and the applicable skeleton stance\n 40|selects the walking-skeleton ceremony, the first in-scope Construction\n 41|EXECUTE stage (`isSkeletonGateStage`) always presents a stage-level approval\n 42|gate regardless of autonomy mode. That gate covers that stage's artifacts\n 43|across the Units that have settled — not a Bolt's combined design artifacts\n 44|and generated code. Audit: emit `GATE_APPROVED` as usual. `BOLT_COMPLETED`\n 45|is not emitted on this gate. Skeleton-off uses the ordinary first-stage gate;\n 46|a zero-Unit stage has no skeleton or Bolt ceremony at all.\n 47|\n 48|> **Planned (non-executable).** A later Bolt-major walk would present a\n 49|> Bolt-level gate covering that Bolt's design artifacts and generated code\n 50|> together, with the enclosing `BOLT_COMPLETED` tying the gate to the Bolt.\n 51|\n 52|**Ladder prompt (fires once, immediately after walking skeleton gate)**\n 53|\n 54|After an actual walking skeleton's gate approves, present exactly one ladder\n 55|prompt. Do not present it for skeleton-off or zero-Unit execution:\n 56|\n 57|```question\n 58|prompt: \"The walking skeleton shipped. How should the remaining Bolts run?\"\n 59|header: Autonomy\n 60|multiSelect: false\n 61|options:\n 62|  - label: Continue autonomously\n 63|    description: Build the remaining Bolts without stopping to check in. I still stop and ask if something fails.\n 64|  - label: Gate every Bolt\n 65|    description: Stop for your approval after each Bolt (or each parallel batch).\n 66|```\n 67|\n 68|The shipped option labels still say \"remaining Bolts\" / \"Gate every Bolt\"; they govern remaining Construction *stage* gates, not Bolt-level gates.\n 69|\n 70|- Record the answer in `aidlc-state.md` as `Construction Autonomy Mode: autonomous` or `Construction Autonomy Mode: gated` via `aidlc-bolt.ts set-autonomy --mode <choice>` (which emits `AUTONOMY_MODE_SET` itself).\n 71|- The ladder choice is set-autonomy-owned, like an approval choice is report-owned: do NOT call `aidlc-log.ts decision` or `aidlc-log.ts answer` for it. Switching to `autonomous` requires the human's fresh turn (the ladder answer) — logging the choice as an interview answer first would consume that turn and the mode switch would refuse.\n 72|- On the default walk, `autonomous` skips the remaining Construction stage gates except halt-and-ask, the Build-and-Test loop-back's rung 4, and the swarm settle `gate: true` re-entry (the conductor auto-approves that settle under autonomy).\n 73|- Session resume: if `Construction Autonomy Mode: unset` but the walking skeleton is already `[x]` complete, re-fire the ladder prompt before executing the next Construction stage.\n 74|\n 75|**Subsequent Bolt gate (per autonomy mode)**\n 76|\n 77|> **Planned (non-executable).** Under a later Bolt-major walk, Bolts after the\n 78|> walking skeleton would present a Bolt-level gate only if `Construction\n 79|> Autonomy Mode: gated`. In `autonomous` mode that gate would be skipped. For\n 80|> parallel Bolt batches the gate would cover every Bolt in the batch. The\n 81|> shipped walk does not present subsequent Bolt-level gates.\n 82|\n 83|**Halt-and-ask on failure**\n 84|\n 85|When Code Generation returns failure, **always halt and present the halt-and-ask prompt regardless of autonomy mode**. This is one of two cases where `autonomous` mode stops to consult the user — the other is the Build-and-Test failure loop-back's rung 4 (below: \"Build-and-Test failure loop-back (3.6 → 3.5)\"), which halts when the loop-back bound is exhausted or no identifiable fix exists.\n 86|\n 87|- Solo Unit failure: halt immediately; on the swarm / worktree path emit `BOLT_FAILED` (with `--slug` for halt-and-ask correlation), present retry / skip / abort.\n 88|- Parallel batch partial failure: wait for all parallel Tasks to return, preserve successful Units' artifacts, emit `BOLT_FAILED` for the failed Unit with `Succeeded=[names]`, present `\"Units [X, Y] succeeded, Unit [Z] failed with: [error]. Options: retry Z, skip Z, abort Construction.\"`\n 89|- Retry: re-run the failed Unit only inside the existing worktree.\n 90|- Skip: mark `[S]` in state with reason, proceed to next batch. Worktree at `<path>` is preserved.\n 91|- Abort: stop Construction; user can resume later. Worktree at `<path>` is preserved.\n 92|\n 93|The orchestrator runs `bun .devin/tools/aidlc.ts engine worktree info --slug <slug>` to obtain the worktree `<path>` and `<branch_name>` deterministically before composing the halt-and-ask question. See `SKILL.md` § \"Halt-and-ask failure handling\" for the full tool-call sequence and the `worktree-info-schema.md` knowledge file for the JSON contract.\n 94|\n 95|```question\n 96|prompt: \"Bolt [Z] failed during code generation: [short error]. Worktree at [path] on branch [branch_name]. How would you like to proceed?\"\n 97|header: Bolt Failure\n 98|multiSelect: false\n 99|options:\n100|  - label: Retry\n101|    description: Re-run Bolt [Z] in the existing worktree.\n102|  - label: Skip\n103|    description: Mark Bolt [Z] skipped; worktree preserved.\n104|  - label: Abort\n105|    description: Stop Construction; worktree preserved.\n106|```\n107|\n108|### Build-and-Test failure loop-back (3.6 → 3.5)\n109|\n110|When Build and Test (3.6) diagnoses a failure whose ROOT CAUSE lies in the\n111|generated code or an approach chosen at code-generation (not in this stage's\n112|own test/build scaffolding), the workflow may return to code-generation and\n113|repair it rather than writing the approach off or dead-ending at the gate.\n114|The stage's Step 9 failure-escalation ladder decides WHEN this fires; this\n115|subsection defines HOW. It is a sanctioned exception to the NO EMERGENT\n116|BEHAVIOR RULE (like the revision escape hatch) and to Critical-checklist\n117|item 5's \"complete the current stage before jumping\": a failed build-and-test\n118|run is deliberately left in-flight — its gate is NOT presented and its §13\n119|learnings ritual DEFERS to the eventual passing run (the stage diary\n120|memory.md persists across the loop).\n121|\n122|**The loop-back counter** lives in test-results.md under `## Loop-Back Log`:\n123|the count of `### Loop-back N` entries IS the bound (max 3 per intent). This\n124|artifact ledger is chosen over parsing STAGE_JUMPED audit rows because it\n125|survives the backward jump (jumps reset checkboxes, never artifacts), is\n126|colocated with the diagnosis it must carry anyway, and is readable at the\n127|final gate; the STAGE_JUMPED rows the jump tool emits remain the\n128|deterministic audit cross-check. The log is append-only. A human-directed\n129|backward jump does not count against the bound — only entries this protocol\n130|writes do.\n131|\n132|**Plan approval on replay.** The jump opens a new stage attempt, so the prior\n133|Plan Approval receipt (bound to the previous attempt) cannot authorize the\n134|replay. Preserve the\n135|Loop-Back Log, but blank `[Answer]:`, regenerate the target-bound fingerprint,\n136|and run Code Generation's Plan Approval decision/human-turn/answer receipt\n137|sequence again before generation. The human's \"Retry with fix\" choice authorizes\n138|the loop-back jump; it is not approval of plan content the human has not\n139|reviewed under the new attempt.\n140|\n141|**Autonomous loop-back procedure** (mode `autonomous`, bound not exhausted,\n142|impact-estimated fix identified):\n143|1. Append the `### Loop-back N — <ISO timestamp>` entry (Diagnosis /\n144|   Root-cause stage / Planned fix / Estimated impact) to test-results.md and a matching\n145|   Deviations entry to this stage's memory.md.\n146|2. Execute the jump through the ENGINE: run\n147|   `bun .devin/tools/aidlc-orchestrate.ts next --stage code-generation`.\n148|   The engine validates the target and answers with a `print` directive naming\n149|   the exact `aidlc-jump.ts execute --target code-generation --direction\n150|   backward --scope <scope>` command; run that printed command verbatim (it\n151|   resets the target + downstream stages, emits the canonical `STAGE_JUMPED`,\n152|   and pivots Current Stage), then re-run `next` and continue the forwarding\n153|   loop. Never compose the `execute` call by hand — the engine's print is the\n154|   validated form.\n155|3. On the code-generation re-entry, follow \"Re-entry settlement and review\"\n156|   below. Before any fix generation, run the fresh target-bound Plan Approval\n157|   sequence required above; this is a human hard stop even though Construction\n158|   autonomy remains granted. Then apply the planned fix ONLY to the unit(s) the diagnosis names and\n159|   apply the deterministic Artifact Re-use decisions (see \"Autonomous failure\n160|   loop-back\" under Artifact Re-use in stage-protocol.md). The standing\n161|   `Construction Autonomy Mode: autonomous` grant is unchanged by the jump;\n162|   after every applicable unit has a fresh current-attempt review, the replayed\n163|   completion gate is auto-approved under it with\n164|   `--user-input \"Autonomous loop-back N per construction protocol module\"` —\n165|   the human already approved the original run of this stage; the replay is a\n166|   repair of that approved shape, not a new autonomy inference (checklist item\n167|   6).\n168|4. Build and Test then re-runs naturally on the forward replay; choose Modify\n169|   at its own Artifact Re-use prompt (never Redo — it would erase the\n170|   Loop-Back Log) and re-execute Step 9 fresh.\n171|\n172|**Re-entry settlement and review.** Backward jumps preserve artifacts, but the\n173|route depends on whether code-generation has ever used the unit lifecycle\n174|ledger:\n175|\n176|1. **Artifact-only workflow** — when no code-generation lifecycle row has ever\n177|   been emitted, artifacts remain the settlement signal. Reviewer Invocation\n  6|\n  7|If the `run-stage` directive includes a `reviewer` field (non-null), the orchestrator MUST invoke the reviewer as a **separate sub-agent** after the stage body produces its artifacts and before the §13 learnings ritual.\n  8|\n  9|The directive's `review_class` field tells you HOW the review runs - the engine has already resolved it (stage declaration, lowered by the scope's `review_cap` and any per-run `--review` override; a `none` resolution omits the reviewer block entirely, so a directive that carries a reviewer always carries a class):\n 10|\n 11|- **`adversarial`** - the refute-and-repair loop below, up to `reviewer_max_iterations` passes with lead fixes between them. For a per-unit\n 42|   `workspace_requires` stage it validates `source-manifest.json` and binds both\n 43|   its bytes and the currently claimed source bytes into `REVIEW_REQUESTED`; it\n 44|   refuses before dispatch when the manifest is missing or invalid. Nobody writes to it\n 54|   during a review; no produces-list position, plugin-added output, or\n 55|   directory enumeration may redefine it. On a per-unit review it resolves\n 56|   inside that Unit.\n 57|\n 58|   On a re-dispatch (adversarial iteration greater than 1, a Part 0 revision\n 59|   re-review, or stale-receipt recovery), run\n 60|   `bun .devin/tools/aidlc-review-brief.ts context --stage \"<directive.stage>\"`;\n 61|   add `--unit \"<directive.unit>\"` on a per-unit review. Retain the complete\n 62|   stdout as `Prior findings (carry IDs forward)` for the dispatch brief. The\n 63|   tool renders the previous review record (or a legacy embedded section) with\n 64|   durable human dispositions from the audit ledger overlaid, so `Accepted\n 65|   risk` and `Rejected: <reason>` survive without touching any artifact.\n 66|\n 67|   Then delegate to the reviewer agent named in `directive.reviewer`. The\n 68|   request remains unmatched while the reviewer runs, so the approval gate and\n 69|   completion stay blocked.\n 70|\n 71|   Pass:\n 72|   - The stage definition file path (`directive.stage_file`)\n 73|   - The Q&A file path (e.g., `<record>/<phase>/<stage>/<stage>-questions.md`)\n 74|   - All artifact file paths produced by the stage (the `produces` artifacts)\n 75|   - The `reviewFile` path from the request JSON, as the one file the reviewer writes\n 76|   - On every re-dispatch named above, `Prior findings (carry IDs forward):` followed by the review-context tool output verbatim. The reviewer MUST preserve those IDs and update their statuses rather than replacing or renumbering the prior list.\n 77|   - The resolved paths in `directive.consumes` - all upstream artifacts the stage declares - paths only, per the context-budget rule. This applies to **every** reviewer-bearing stage, not only per-unit ones:\n 78|     - For a **per-unit** stage (`directive.unit` present) these include the shared inception contracts that pin cross-unit boundaries (`components.md`, `contract-summary.md`, `unit-of-work.md`).\n 79|     - For a **workflow-level** stage with no `directive.unit` (e.g. `contract-design`), these are the upstream artifacts that justify the produced output - the unit DAG (`unit-of-work.md`, `unit-of-work-dependency.md`), the component catalogue (`components.md`), and `requirements.md` - so the reviewer can verify the contracts against the boundaries, entities, and NFRs they formalise rather than reviewing the summary in isolation.\n 80|   - The validation tools list from the stage definition's frontmatter (if any)\n 81|   - For a per-unit `workspace_requires` stage, the unit's\n 82|     `source-manifest.json` path and its claimed source paths. Review the\n 83|     implementation differentially at those paths rather than sweeping the\n 84|     whole workspace; treat any claim that looks unrelated to the unit as a\n 85|     finding.\n 86|\n 87|   Do NOT pass: `memory.md` (builder's diary) or any plan/reasoning files. The reviewer forms independent judgment.\n 88|\n 89|   **Reviewer read scope.** The reviewer's scope is the current unit's artifacts plus the passed contract paths. On a per-unit stage the reviewer MUST NOT read other units' `construction/<other-unit>/` content through any tool - not by opening files, and not via grep, glob, or shell patterns that span sibling unit paths (a `construction/*/` glob is a sibling read, not a search) - except to spot-check an integration point the current unit's design explicitly names, and only the owning file, resolved via the shared contracts rather than by browsing or searching the sibling's directory. Cross-unit contract verification runs against the shared inception artifacts passed above, not against a sweep of sibling units' design prose.\n 90|\n 91|   **Dispatch record (per-unit stages; enforcement-capable harnesses only).** This record is required only when the current harness registers reviewer-scope PreToolUse enforcement (Claude Code, Kiro CLI, Codex CLI, opencode, Cursor, and GitHub Copilot today). Immediately before invoking a per-unit reviewer (`directive.unit` present) on one of those harnesses, write `<record>/.aidlc-reviewer-dispatch.json`:\n 92|\n 93|   ```json\n 94|   {\"reviewer\": \"<directive.reviewer>\", \"stage\": \"<stage slug>\", \"unit\": \"<directive.unit>\",\n 95|    \"exempt\": [\"<each resolved directive.consumes path>\", \"<stage file path>\", \"<Q&A file path>\"]}\n 96|   ```\n 97|\n 98|   When the current unit's design explicitly names an integration point in a sibling unit's file, resolve that single owning file via the shared contracts and append its path to `exempt` - the record is where the spot-check carve-out is granted. The `stage` field appears verbatim in any `REVIEWER_SCOPE_BLOCKED` audit row; use the current stage slug. The reviewer-scope PreToolUse hook reads this record to enforce the read-scope bound deterministically while the review is in flight; on a NOT-READY re-invoke (step 3 back to step 1), write a fresh record. Single-stage reviews (no `directive.unit`) write no record. On a harness without reviewer-scope enforcement (Kiro IDE today), do not write the record; the reviewer read-scope bound remains mandatory prose in the delegated task and reviewer persona.\n 99|\n100|   If that dispatch fails, times out, or ends without a recorded verdict - the\n101|   session died, or the reviewer returned an incomplete attempt (step 3: no\n102|   review file, or one without a single canonical verdict) - return to the\n103|   start of this step and rerun the same request command with `--retry-pending`\n104|   immediately before dispatching again. The logger accepts it exactly once,\n105|   only while that exact request is unmatched and the declared artifacts and\n106|   workspace source exactly match the original request; it reuses those\n107|   original fingerprints and request id instead of rebaselining current bytes,\n108|   marks the retry in the audit, reopens the review slot, and does not consume\n109|   another review iteration. Never use `--retry-pending` after a verdict; a\n110|   receipt-invalidating write creates a new recovery request at the next\n111|   ordinal, not a retry of the completed one.\n112|2. **Reviewer executes.** An `adversarial` review runs under the **adversarial review contract**:\n113|\n114|   - **Refute, don't confirm.** The reviewer's job is to refute the artifact, not to confirm it. It assumes defects exist and hunts for them; READY is the verdict it fails to reach after trying to break the artifact, not the default it starts from.\n115|   - **Ground findings in machine-checkable evidence where it exists.** The reviewer runs the validation tools the invocation lists (via shell) and checks the artifact against its acceptance criteria, its stage definition, and the consumed upstream contracts. A finding backed only by opinion is a suggestion, not grounds for NOT-READY.\n116|\n117|   An `advisory` review keeps the evidence-grounding rule but not the refute-until-READY posture: tell the reviewer in the dispatch brief that this is a SINGLE normal-flow advisory pass whose findings go to the human at the approval gate - report only findings the human should weigh before approving, ranked by severity, with no fix-and-re-review loop behind it. The stale-receipt recovery below is a separate bounded request, not a repair loop.\n118|\n119|   The reviewer sub-agent:\n120|   - Reads the stage definition to understand what SHOULD have been produced\n121|   - Reads the Q&A to understand context and constraints\n122|   - Reads the artifact(s) to evaluate what WAS produced\n123|   - Verifies cross-unit contract claims against the passed shared inception contracts, not by sweeping or searching sibling units' design directories (no cross-unit grep or glob patterns); opens another unit's file only when the current unit's design explicitly names it as an integration point, and only that file\n124|   - Runs any validation tools listed (via shell) and includes results in findings\n125|   - Writes exactly ONE file: its review, at the passed `reviewFile` path. The review uses the knowledge template and contains exactly one rendered `**Verdict:** READY|NOT-READY`, one rendered `**Reviewer:** <directive.reviewer>`, and one rendered `**Iteration:** <n>` line, with its findings under `### Findings` in the template's table. It may open with the template's `## Review` heading and use H3+ subsections, but no later H1, H2, setext, or raw-HTML H1/H2 heading may open unowned top-level content. Literal headings and ownership-field examples inside fenced or inline code do not count. Step 3 treats anything else as an incomplete review.\n126|   - Writes NOTHING else: not the reviewed artifact, not any other `produces[]` output, not `source-manifest.json`, not a claimed source path. The bytes the reviewer was dispatched on are the bytes the verdict certifies, and the logger refuses a verdict whose artifacts changed.\n127|   - Returns a response whose FIRST line is its identity marker verbatim\n128|     (`**Reviewer:** <reviewer-agent-name>`), so the `SUBAGENT_COMPLETED` audit\n129|     event records which reviewer ran. The reviewer's persona owns this contract.\n130|\n131|   When the review artifact is also the subject of a Plan Approval (Code Generation\n132|   declares `code-generation-plan` as both), recording the review does NOT touch\n133|   the plan, so the approval is unaffected. The approval fingerprint still\n134|   projects out a terminal `## Review` section left in the plan by a review\n135|   recorded before review records existed; nothing new is written there.\n136|\n137|3. **Read verdict.** After the reviewer returns, delete `<record>/.aidlc-reviewer-dispatch.json` if one was written (the enforcement window closes with the review; a leftover record would keep refusing sibling access for later, unrelated work), then record the terminal receipt with the same `aidlc-log.ts review` command plus `--verdict <READY|NOT-READY>` (and the same `--unit` / `--single` fields). The logger reads the review from the request's `reviewFile` (pass `--review-file <path>` to name another file), validates it with Bun's Markdown parser (fenced/inline code and HTML comments cannot supply or conflict with authority fields, list/blockquote/table containers cannot mint ownership, and rendered Markdown or raw-HTML H1/H2 headings are section escapes), proves from one coherent snapshot that every dispatched artifact byte and the request-time source identity are unchanged, and then writes the review record `<record>/.aidlc-reviews/<stage>/stage/<attempt>/<iteration>.json` (or the Unit path under `units/<unit>/`) (verdict, findings, reviewer, request id, artifact and source fingerprints, and the review text) in the same locked transaction as the `REVIEW_COMPLETED` row that names the record and pins its digest. The record is the review; only this command writes one, and a record edited afterwards stops being the review because its digest no longer matches. The command's JSON returns `reviewRecord`, the record's path relative to the intent record.\n138|\n139|   Anything else is an INCOMPLETE attempt, not a verdict: no review file at all (the reviewer has a hard turn cap and may have been stopped before writing it; the request opened an empty slot, so a missing file means an incomplete review on every path, first entry or revision alike), a review with no canonical verdict line or one that does not match `--verdict`, forged/missing/conflicting duplicate ownership fields, a later top-level heading, or a malformed findings table. The logger refuses these; a malformed audit `REVIEW_COMPLETED` row is ignored and does not consume the pending request.\n140|\n141|   **On an incomplete attempt:** no verdict exists to record, so the step-1\n142|   request is still unmatched. If the ledger does not yet mark a retry on this\n143|   request, re-dispatch it exactly once - return to step 1 and rerun the same\n144|   request command with `--retry-pending` immediately before dispatch. The\n145|   logger accepts this only while the request is unmatched, has not already\n146|   spent its retry, and the original artifact and source bytes are unchanged;\n147|   it consumes no review iteration and never mints a new fingerprint. A valid\n148|   unmatched request recorded before review records (or before source binding)\n149|   may emit exactly one audit-marked `Upgrade: legacy-request` modern binding,\n150|   but only while its recorded artifact fingerprint still matches; the reviewer\n151|   MUST then be freshly dispatched. A field-light historical\n152|   `Retry: pending-request` marker is not a modern binding and therefore does\n153|   not block that one modernization, while the modern upgrade row itself spends\n154|   the retry and blocks every later retry. A structurally malformed request row\n155|   has no authority and is ignored, so a fresh normal request may reuse its\n156|   ordinal. If the retried attempt is ALSO incomplete, stop retrying: record the\n157|   terminal receipt with `--verdict NOT-READY` and no review file; the logger\n158|   accepts a missing review only for this retried NOT-READY fallback, and\n159|   writes an empty review record for it. Recording the receipt is what keeps the engine's gate and\n169|   completion precondition satisfiable: the gate is never presented on a\n170|   silently missing verdict, and never deadlocks on one either.\n171|\n172|   **Migration (deprecated).** A review embedded as a terminal `## Review`\n173|   section in `directive.review_artifact` is still readable: the gate brief and\n174|   the redispatch context render it when no record exists for that scope. The tool reads markdown files from an input directory, converts them to HTML, and writes a static site to an output directory. It supports a `build` command and a `serve` command (live preview dev server).\n\n**Key dependencies:**\n- `marked` — CommonMark + GFM markdown parsing\n- `gray-matter` — YAML frontmatter extraction\n- `highlight.js` — syntax highlighting for fenced code blocks\n- `commander` — CLI command/flag parsing\n- `chokidar` — file watching for dev server\n- `vitest` — test runner\n\n**Source layout:**\n```\nsrc/\n  types.ts            — shared types (PageMetadata, SiteConfig, NavItem)\n  frontmatter.ts      — YAML frontmatter extraction (FR2)\n  markdown.ts         — markdown-to-HTML conversion with GFM + highlighting (FR1)\n  site-generator.ts   — orchestrates full site build from input to output (FR3, FR4)\n  navigation.ts       — sidebar nav + index page generation (FR3)\n  assets.ts           — static asset copying + CSS handling (FR4)\n  dev-server.ts       — live preview HTTP server with watch + reload (FR5)\n  cli.ts              — CLI entry point with build/serve commands (FR6)\n  templates.ts        — HTML page template rendering\ntests/\n  frontmatter.test.ts\n  markdown.test.ts\n  site-generator.test.ts\n  navigation.test.ts\n  assets.test.ts\n  dev-server.test.ts\n  cli.test.ts\n```\n\n## Implementation Steps\n\n### Step 1: Project structure and production configuration skeleton\n- [ ] Create `package.json` with name `md-to-site`, bin entry, Each command is scoped to specific test files — never run a bare `npm test` that would rerun the whole suite.\n\n**Run all tests for this unit:**\n```bash\nnpx vitest run --reporter verbose\n```\n\n**Run a specific test file:**\n```bash\nnpx vitest run tests/frontmatter.test.ts --reporter verbose\n```\n\n**Run in watch mode (during development):**\n```bash\nnpx vitest\n```\n\nThe exact command that is runnable before the first test-after cycle:\n```bash\nnpx vitest run tests/frontmatter.test.ts --reporter verbose\n```\n\n## Test Files and Coverage\n\n### tests/frontmatter.test.ts (FR2 — Frontmatter Support)\n- Happy path: extract valid YAML frontmatter with title, date, tags, description\n- Edge case: no frontmatter present (returns empty metadata)\n- Edge case: custom fields beyond the standard set\n- Title fallback: filename used when `title` field absent\n- ~5 tests\n\n### tests/markdown.test.ts (FR1 — Markdown Conversion)\n- Happy path: CommonMark syntax converts to correct HTML\n- GFM: tables, task lists, strikethrough, autolinks\n- Code highlighting: fenced code blocks produce highlighted HTML\n- ~5 tests\n\n### descending, alphabetical fallback\n- Internal links: valid links generated between pages\n- ~5 tests\n\n### tests/site-generator.test.ts (FR3, FR4 — Full Build)\n- Happy path: full build from sample tests across 7 test files\n- Every requirement (FR1–FR6) has at least one verifiable test\n\n## Mocking / Stubbing Guidance\n\n- Use `vitest`'s built-in `vi.mock()` for mocking `chokidar` in dev-server tests\n- Use temp directories (`os.tmpdir()`) for file I/O tests — create sample markdown trees, assert output\n- Mock HTTP server where needed using `vi.mock` for `http` module in dev-server tests\n- No external mock libraries needed — Vitest provides mocking natively\n\n## Test Data Management\n\n- Create fixture markdown files in `tests/fixtures/` directory\n- Sample files: `tests/fixtures/sample.md` (with frontmatter), `tests/fixtures/no-frontmatter.md`, `tests/fixtures/subdir/note.md`\n- Frontmatter test data: inline YAML strings in test cases\n- Clean up temp
